| 1949-06-06 | The Senate Foreign Relations Committee reports the treaty favorably by a vote of 13 to 0. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1949-07-21 | The Senate gives its advice and consent to ratification, 82 to 13. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1949-07-25 | President Harry S. Truman ratifies the treaty. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1949-08-24 | The treaty enters into force after the remaining instruments of ratification are deposited. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1949-08-29 | The Soviet Union tests its first atomic bomb, ending the U.S. nuclear monopoly. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1950 | About 1.2 million Black southerners leave per decade through the 1950s and 1960s. — [Great Migration](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/great-migration.md) |
| 1950 | Mullane v. Central Hanover Bank sets minimum requirements of notice, a hearing and an impartial tribunal. — [Due process](../articles/rights-and-law/due-process.md) |
| 1950 | The census records the city's peak population, 3,620,962. — [Chicago](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/chicago.md) |
| 1950-02-09 | Senator Joseph McCarthy tells the Women's Republican Club of Wheeling, West Virginia, that he holds a list of 205 State Department Communists. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1950-06-01 | Senator Margaret Chase Smith of Maine and six other Republicans issue the Declaration of Conscience against the tactics of McCarthyism. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1950-06-25 | North Korean forces cross the 38th parallel; within days Truman orders U.S. forces into South Korea. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1950-09 | Congress passes the McCarran Internal Security Act, requiring communist organizations to register with the government. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1950-12 | General Dwight D. Eisenhower becomes the first Supreme Allied Commander Europe. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1951 | The Bureau of Indian Affairs sets up a Branch of Placement and Relocation to move Native people from reservations to cities. — [Native Americans](../articles/native-nations/native-americans.md) |
| 1951-02-09 | Indicted with officers of the Peace Information Center; acquitted after a trial that November. — [W. E. B. Du Bois](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/w-e-b-du-bois.md) |
| 1951-02-27 | The Twenty-second Amendment is ratified, limiting presidents to two elected terms. — [Presidency of the United States](../articles/constitution-and-government/presidency-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1951-04-23 | Students led by 16-year-old Barbara Rose Johns walk out of the overcrowded Robert Russa Moton High School in Farmville, Virginia. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1951-05-23 | NAACP lawyers file Davis v. County School Board of Prince Edward County, later joined to Brown. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1952 | Greece and Turkey join the alliance. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1952 | The Penn School graduates its last class. — [Gullah Geechee](../articles/society-and-people/gullah-geechee.md) |
| 1952 | The Veterans Readjustment Assistance Act extends education and training benefits to Korean War veterans. — [Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/servicemens-readjustment-act-of-1944.md) |
| 1952-12-09 | The Supreme Court hears the first arguments in the consolidated school cases. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1953 | Congress passes the Outer Continental Shelf Lands Act, affirming federal jurisdiction over offshore mineral resources and putting Interior in charge of leasing. — [Bureau of Ocean Energy Management](../articles/geography-and-environment/bureau-of-ocean-energy-management.md) |
| 1953 | Manufacturing employs 359,000 Philadelphians, 45% of the city's workforce. — [Philadelphia](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/philadelphia.md) |
| 1953 | The American Indian Center of Chicago is founded. — [Chicago](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/chicago.md) |
| 1953-04-27 | President Dwight D. Eisenhower issues Executive Order 10450, which bars gay men and lesbians from federal employment. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1953-06-18 | Marries Coretta Scott in Marion, Alabama. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1953-06-19 | Julius and Ethel Rosenberg are executed for conspiracy to commit espionage. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1953-07-27 | An armistice ends the fighting in Korea. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1953-08-01 | House Concurrent Resolution 108 announces the policy of terminating federal supervision of tribes. — [Tribal sovereignty](../articles/native-nations/tribal-sovereignty.md) |
| 1953-08-15 | Public Law 280 shifts criminal and civil jurisdiction on most reservations in five states to state authorities. — [Tribal sovereignty](../articles/native-nations/tribal-sovereignty.md) |
| 1953-12-07 | Reargument begins on questions the Court posed, including the history of the Fourteenth Amendment and possible remedies. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1954 – 1968 | **[Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md)** (Movement) |
| 1954 | Becomes pastor of Dexter Avenue Baptist Church in Montgomery, Alabama. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1954 | Brown v. Board of Education declares school segregation unconstitutional. — [Jim Crow laws](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/jim-crow-laws.md) |
| 1954 | Brown v. Board of Education holds that segregated public schools violate equal protection. — [Equal protection](../articles/rights-and-law/equal-protection.md) |
| 1954 | Congress adds the words 'under God' to the Pledge of Allegiance. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1954 | Muddy Waters records Willie Dixon's "I'm Your Hoochie Coochie Man." — [Blues](../articles/culture-and-arts/blues.md) |
| 1954 | The NAACP's legal campaign against segregated schools culminates in Brown v. Board of Education. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1954-01 | President Dwight D. Eisenhower recommends an 18-year-old voting amendment in his State of the Union address; the Senate later falls short of the two-thirds needed. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1954-03 | The Women's Political Council meets Mayor W. A. Gayle to ask for changes in how Black riders are treated on city buses. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1954-03-16 | The Senate Permanent Subcommittee on Investigations opens its inquiry into McCarthy's clash with the U.S. Army; the hearings are televised nationally. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1954-05-17 | **[Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md)** (CourtCase) |
| 1954-05-17 | Brown v. Board of Education rejects separate but equal in public education. — [Plessy v. Ferguson](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/plessy-v-ferguson.md) |
| 1954-05-17 | The Court rules unanimously that segregated public schools violate the Equal Protection Clause; Bolling v. Sharpe is decided the same day. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1954-05-17 | The Supreme Court rules in Brown v. Board of Education that segregated public schools are unconstitutional. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1954-05-21 | Council president Jo Ann Robinson writes to the mayor that many local groups have discussed a city-wide boycott. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1954-06-09 | Army counsel Joseph Welch rebukes McCarthy on live television after McCarthy attacks a young lawyer on Welch's staff. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1954-08 | The Communist Control Act effectively criminalizes membership in the Communist Party. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1954-11-23 | The Dow finally regains its 1929 high. — [Stock market crash of 1929](../articles/economy-and-work/stock-market-crash-of-1929.md) |
| 1954-12-02 | The Senate votes 67 to 22 to censure McCarthy. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1955 | Kentucky lowers its voting age to 18 by referendum. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1955 | Receives a doctorate in systematic theology from Boston University. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1955 | The AFL and CIO merge as the AFL-CIO under George Meany. — [Labor movement](../articles/economy-and-work/labor-movement.md) |
| 1955 | The Veterans Administration has guaranteed 4.3 million home loans with a face value of $33 billion. — [Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/servicemens-readjustment-act-of-1944.md) |
| 1955 | West Germany joins; the Soviet Union answers with its own alliance of Eastern European states, the Warsaw Pact. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1955-03-02 | Fifteen-year-old Claudette Colvin is removed from a Montgomery bus and arrested for defying segregation. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1955-05-24 | The Little Rock School Board adopts Superintendent Virgil Blossom's gradual plan, beginning with token desegregation of Central High in 1957. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1955-05-31 | In Brown II the Court orders desegregation to proceed with all deliberate speed under federal district courts. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1955-12 | Chosen to lead the Montgomery Improvement Association after Rosa Parks's arrest; the FBI begins monitoring him. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1955-12-01 | Rosa Parks is arrested for refusing to give up her seat to a white passenger. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1955-12-01 | Rosa Parks is arrested in Montgomery, Alabama, setting off the Montgomery bus boycott. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1955-12-05 – 1956-12-20 | **[Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md)** (Event) |
| 1955-12-05 | About 90 percent of Black riders stay off the buses; the Montgomery Improvement Association forms and elects Martin Luther King Jr. president. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1955-12-08 | The MIA presents its demands: courtesy, first-come seating and Black drivers on mainly Black routes. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956 | The education and training program for World War II veterans ends after paying out $14.5 billion. — [Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/servicemens-readjustment-act-of-1944.md) |
| 1956-01-30 | Segregationists bomb King's house. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-02-01 | Fred Gray and Charles D. Langford file Browder v. Gayle in federal court. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-03 | The State Department sends Dizzy Gillespie on the first of its Cold War jazz-ambassador tours. — [Jazz](../articles/culture-and-arts/jazz.md) |
| 1956-03-12 | The Southern Manifesto denouncing the decision is introduced in Congress. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1956-03-22 | King is convicted under a 1921 anti-boycott law and fined $500 or 386 days in jail. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-04-24 | Alaska voters adopt a state constitution. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1956-06-05 | A three-judge federal court rules 2–1 that bus segregation is unconstitutional. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-07-30 | An act of Congress declares 'In God we trust' the national motto; E pluribus unum stays on the seal and coins. — [E pluribus unum](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/e-pluribus-unum.md) |
| 1956-07-30 | Congress declares 'In God we trust' the national motto. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1956-11-13 | The U.S. Supreme Court affirms the district court's ruling. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-12-17 | The Supreme Court rejects city and state requests to reconsider. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-12-20 | The Court's order reaches Montgomery and the MIA votes to end the boycott. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1956-12-21 | Montgomery's buses operate on an integrated basis. — [Montgomery bus boycott](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/montgomery-bus-boycott.md) |
| 1957 | Founds the Southern Christian Leadership Conference and becomes its president. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1957 | The Soviet Union launches Sputnik 1, the first artificial satellite to orbit Earth. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1957-01 | More than sixty ministers meet at Ebenezer Baptist Church in Atlanta and form the Southern Christian Leadership Conference. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1957-05 | McCarthy dies at age 48. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1957-08-28 | Federal judge Ronald Davies voids a state court injunction against integration and orders the district to proceed. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-02 – 1958-05 | **[Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md)** (Event) |
| 1957-09-02 | Governor Orval Faubus announces on television that he has called out the Arkansas National Guard. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-04 | Guardsmen turn the Black students away; fifteen-year-old Elizabeth Eckford walks alone past a jeering crowd. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-14 | Faubus and Eisenhower meet at Newport, Rhode Island, without reaching agreement. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-20 | Judge Davies orders Faubus and the Guard to stop interfering, and Faubus withdraws the troops. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-23 | The students enter Central, but a mob of more than 1,000 forms outside and they are taken out by lunchtime; Eisenhower issues Proclamation 3204. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-24 | Eisenhower signs Executive Order 10730, federalizing the Arkansas Guard and sending in the 101st Airborne Division. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-09-25 | Escorted by federal troops, the Little Rock Nine attend their first full day of classes. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1957-10-04 | The Soviet Union launches Sputnik 1, the first artificial satellite; NASA and ARPA are created the next year. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1957-11 | The 101st Airborne returns to Fort Campbell, leaving federalized Guard units in charge for the rest of the year. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1958 | Cooper v. Aaron invokes Marbury as the foundation of judicial supremacy, cementing its modern image. — [Marbury v. Madison](../articles/rights-and-law/marbury-v-madison.md) |
| 1958 | Hansen supervises a rebuilding of the terrazzo floor. — [Winged Figures of the Republic](../articles/culture-and-arts/winged-figures-of-the-republic.md) |
| 1958-02 | Minnijean Brown is expelled after retaliating against harassment. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1958-05 | Ernest Green becomes the first Black student to graduate from Central High. — [Desegregation of Little Rock Central High School](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/desegregation-of-little-rock-central-high-school.md) |
| 1958-07-07 | President Eisenhower signs the Alaska Statehood Act after House passage on May 28 and Senate passage on June 30. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1958-09-29 | In Cooper v. Aaron all nine justices declare the federal judiciary supreme in expounding the Constitution and reject Arkansas's resistance to desegregation. — [Judicial review](../articles/constitution-and-government/judicial-review.md) |
| 1959 | Alaska and Hawaii join the Union with voting ages of 19 and 20. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1959 | The introduced oyster parasite MSX appears in the bay. — [Chesapeake Bay](../articles/geography-and-environment/chesapeake-bay.md) |
| 1959-01-03 | **[Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md)** (State) |
| 1959-01-03 | Alaska becomes a state. — [United States](../articles/states-and-territories/united-states.md) |
| 1959-01-03 | Eisenhower proclaims Alaska's admission as the forty-ninth state. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1959-01-12 | Berry Gordy Jr. founds Tamla Records, soon joined by the Motown label. — [Detroit](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/detroit.md) |
| 1959-03-18 | President Eisenhower signs the statehood act passed by Congress. — [Hawaii](../articles/states-and-territories/hawaii.md) |
| 1959-06-26 | Prince Edward County, Virginia, cuts off public school funding rather than desegregate. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1959-08-21 | **[Hawaii](../articles/states-and-territories/hawaii.md)** (State) |
| 1959-08-21 | Eisenhower proclaims Hawaii the fiftieth state, after island voters accept statehood in a June referendum. — [Hawaii](../articles/states-and-territories/hawaii.md) |
| 1959-08-21 | Hawaii becomes the fiftieth state. — [United States](../articles/states-and-territories/united-states.md) |
| 1960 | Discussions in Princeton's experimental gravitation research group plant the idea of ranging to a reflector on the Moon. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1960-02-01 | Four North Carolina A&T students sit in at a segregated Woolworth's lunch counter in Greensboro; sit-ins spread across the South. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1960-04 | Students found the Student Nonviolent Coordinating Committee at a conference at Shaw University organized by Ella Baker. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1960-04 | With Ella Baker, convenes the student conference at Shaw University that founds SNCC; moves to Atlanta that year as co-pastor of Ebenezer Baptist Church. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1960-07-04 | The 50-star flag becomes official after Hawaii's admission as a state. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1961 | Larry Gara's The Liberty Line challenges the legend and recentres freedom seekers themselves. — [Underground Railroad](../articles/civil-war-and-reconstruction/underground-railroad.md) |
| 1961-04 | Soviet cosmonaut Yuri Gagarin becomes the first person to orbit Earth. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1961-05-04 | Thirteen Black and white Freedom Riders leave Washington, D.C., to test the desegregation of interstate bus travel. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1961-05-25 | President John F. Kennedy asks Congress to commit to a crewed Moon landing before the decade is out. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1961-08 | Young Jefferson County lawyers file suit in the U.S. District Court for the Middle District of Alabama, with M. O. Sims among the plaintiffs. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1961-09 | Congress honors Samuel Wilson of Troy, New York, as the progenitor of Uncle Sam. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1961-09-03 | Amendments extending coverage to large retail and service enterprises take effect. — [Minimum wage](../articles/economy-and-work/minimum-wage.md) |
| 1961-10-07 | Leaves for Ghana to direct the Encyclopedia Africana project, days after applying to join the Communist Party. — [W. E. B. Du Bois](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/w-e-b-du-bois.md) |
| 1961-12-01 | The final original generating unit, N-8, goes online. — [Hoover Dam](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/hoover-dam.md) |
| 1961-12-11 | Martin Luther King Jr. addresses the AFL-CIO convention and urges it to fight discrimination. — [Labor movement](../articles/economy-and-work/labor-movement.md) |
| 1962 | California passes New York as the most populous state. — [California](../articles/states-and-territories/california.md) |
| 1962 | In Baker v. Carr the Supreme Court holds that challenges to legislative apportionment can be heard by federal courts. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1962 | John F. Kennedy invokes the doctrine during the Cuban Missile Crisis and imposes a quarantine on Cuba with OAS support. — [Monroe Doctrine](../articles/early-republic-and-expansion/monroe-doctrine.md) |
| 1962 | The USS Arizona Memorial, built over the sunken battleship, is officially dedicated. — [Attack on Pearl Harbor](../articles/depression-and-world-war/attack-on-pearl-harbor.md) |
| 1962-05 | Judges Frank M. Johnson and Richard T. Rives rule for the plaintiffs, and the court orders a temporary plan for the 1962 election. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1962-09-12 | Kennedy defends the Moon goal in a speech at Rice University. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1962-10-22 | Kennedy announces a naval quarantine of Cuba after U-2 photographs reveal Soviet missile sites. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1962-10-28 | Khrushchev announces that the Soviet missiles in Cuba will be dismantled and removed. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1963 | In Arizona v. California the Supreme Court settles the Lower Basin division and recognizes reserved water rights for five reservations. — [Colorado River](../articles/geography-and-environment/colorado-river.md) |
| 1963 | In Gray v. Sanders the Court strikes down Georgia's county-unit system, declaring the principle of one person, one vote. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1963-04 | Arrested during SCLC's Birmingham campaign; writes his Letter from Birmingham Jail. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1963-08-27 | Dies in Ghana as a Ghanaian citizen, the day before the March on Washington. — [W. E. B. Du Bois](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/w-e-b-du-bois.md) |
| 1963-08-28 | More than 200,000 people join the March on Washington for Jobs and Freedom. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1963-08-28 | Speaks at the March on Washington for Jobs and Freedom before more than 200,000 people. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1963-10 | Attorney General Robert Kennedy authorizes FBI wiretaps on King's home and SCLC offices. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1963-11-13 | The Supreme Court hears oral argument in Reynolds v. Sims. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1964 | A riot over flying Panama's flag in the Canal Zone briefly breaks U.S.-Panamanian relations. — [Panama Canal](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/panama-canal.md) |
| 1964 | B.B. King records the album Live at the Regal at Chicago's Regal Theater. — [Blues](../articles/culture-and-arts/blues.md) |
| 1964 | In Wesberry v. Sanders the Court requires congressional districts to be as equal in population as practicable. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1964-03-27 | **[1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md)** (Event) |
| 1964-03-27 | A magnitude 9.2 earthquake, the largest recorded in U.S. history, and its tsunamis kill 129 people. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1964-03-27 | About four hours after the quake the open-ocean tsunami reaches Oregon; it kills four children at Beverly Beach and 12 people at Crescent City, California. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1964-03-27 | At 5:36 p.m. local time the Aleutian megathrust ruptures beneath Prince William Sound; shaking lasts several minutes. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1964-03-27 | Underwater landslides collapse the Valdez waterfront and send waves into Seward, Whittier and Chenega; the Turnagain Heights slide destroys about 75 homes in Anchorage. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1964-03-28 | President Johnson declares Alaska a disaster area and directs federal agencies and the military to assist. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1964-04-02 | Executive Order 11150 creates the Federal Reconstruction and Development Planning Commission for Alaska. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1964-06-15 | **[Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md)** (CourtCase) |
| 1964-06-15 | The Court decides Reynolds 8 to 1 along with companion cases from New York, Maryland, Virginia, Delaware and Colorado. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1964-06-21 | Mississippi Freedom Summer begins; James Chaney, Andrew Goodman and Michael Schwerner are murdered by Klansmen in Neshoba County. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1964-07-02 | President Lyndon B. Johnson signs the Civil Rights Act of 1964. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1964-07-02 | The Civil Rights Act outlaws segregation in businesses such as theaters, restaurants and hotels. — [Jim Crow laws](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/jim-crow-laws.md) |
| 1964-07-19 | Cahokia Mounds is designated a National Historic Landmark. — [Cahokia](../articles/native-nations/cahokia.md) |
| 1964-09-08 | Prince Edward County's public schools reopen after the Supreme Court's Griffin decision. — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1964-10-20 | Dies in New York City at age 90; buried at West Branch, Iowa. — [Herbert Hoover](../articles/presidents-and-founders/herbert-hoover.md) |
| 1964-12 | Receives the Nobel Peace Prize. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1965 | The Immigration and Nationality Act ends the national-origins quota system. — [Chinese Exclusion Act](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/chinese-exclusion-act.md) |
| 1965 | The Immigration and Nationality Act makes family reunification the main basis for admission, opening the way for large-scale immigration from China, Hong Kong and Taiwan. — [Chinese Americans](../articles/society-and-people/chinese-americans.md) |
| 1965 | The Kinzua Dam on the Allegheny River is completed, flooding about 10,000 acres of the Seneca Allegany Reservation and forcing about 700 people to move. — [Haudenosaunee](../articles/native-nations/haudenosaunee.md) |
| 1965 | The Voting Rights Act and Great Society grant programs expand the federal role under cooperative federalism. — [Federalism](../articles/constitution-and-government/federalism.md) |
| 1965 | The Voting Rights Act's Section 4 formula covers Alabama, Georgia, Louisiana, Mississippi, South Carolina and Virginia, plus 39 North Carolina counties and one Arizona county. — [Shelby County v. Holder](../articles/democracy-and-elections/shelby-county-v-holder.md) |
| 1965 | USGS geologist George Plafker publishes his map of uplift and subsidence across south-central Alaska in Science. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1965-02-26 | Voting rights activist Jimmie Lee Jackson dies of a gunshot wound inflicted by a white man in Alabama, prompting calls for a march from Selma to Montgomery. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-03-07 | On "Bloody Sunday," law enforcement officers attack about 600 marchers leaving Selma for Montgomery. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-03-07 | State troopers and local officers attack about 600 voting-rights marchers on the Edmund Pettus Bridge in Selma, Alabama (Bloody Sunday). — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1965-03-15 | President Johnson outlines his voting rights proposals in a televised address to a joint session of Congress. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-03-15 | President Lyndon B. Johnson asks Congress for a law to make the amendment impossible to evade. — [Fifteenth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/fifteenth-amendment.md) |
| 1965-03-25 | Addresses marchers at the Alabama capitol in Montgomery after the Selma-to-Montgomery march. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1965-03-25 | Some 25,000 marchers led by Martin Luther King Jr. reach Montgomery under federal protection. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-05-26 | The Senate passes S. 1564, 77 to 19, a day after invoking cloture 70 to 30 to end a filibuster. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-07-09 | The House passes H.R. 6400, 333 to 85. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-08-04 | A proposed constitutional amendment to let one legislative house be apportioned on factors besides population wins 57 to 39 in the Senate, short of two-thirds. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1965-08-06 | **[Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md)** (Law) |
| 1965-08-06 | Johnson signs the Voting Rights Act at the Capitol, a day after Congress completed final passage. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1965-08-06 | Johnson signs the Voting Rights Act of 1965. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1965-08-06 | President Johnson signs the Voting Rights Act. — [Jim Crow laws](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/jim-crow-laws.md) |
| 1965-09-08 | More than 800 Filipino farmworkers in AWOC begin the Delano grape strike; César Chávez's NFWA votes to join on September 16. — [Labor movement](../articles/economy-and-work/labor-movement.md) |
| 1966 | César Chávez and Dolores Huerta found the United Farm Workers to organize mostly Mexican American farmworkers. — [California](../articles/states-and-territories/california.md) |
| 1966 | Harper v. Virginia State Board of Elections holds Virginia's poll tax unconstitutional. — [Jim Crow laws](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/jim-crow-laws.md) |
| 1966 | In South Carolina v. Katzenbach the Supreme Court upholds the Act's constitutionality. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1966 | Moves into a Chicago neighborhood to campaign against poverty and housing discrimination. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1966 | The Veterans Readjustment Benefits Act provides education benefits and home and farm loans for Vietnam-era veterans. — [Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/servicemens-readjustment-act-of-1944.md) |
| 1966-06-16 | Stokely Carmichael of SNCC makes Black Power a public slogan at a rally in Greenwood, Mississippi. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1966-08-26 | The Chicago Freedom Movement reaches an open-housing summit agreement with city officials and lenders. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1967 | Melvin Fowler begins excavating Cahokia's Mound 72, where a burial holds a cache of 15 polished chunkey stones. — [Chunkey](../articles/native-nations/chunkey.md) |
| 1967 | The West Coast/Alaska Tsunami Warning Center is founded at Palmer because of the earthquake (renamed the National Tsunami Warning Center in 2013). — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1967-02-01 | Under the 1966 amendments, farmworkers are covered by the federal minimum for the first time, at $1.00 an hour. — [Minimum wage](../articles/economy-and-work/minimum-wage.md) |
| 1967-02-10 | The Twenty-fifth Amendment is ratified, setting rules for succession and presidential disability. — [Presidency of the United States](../articles/constitution-and-government/presidency-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1967-04-04 | Denounces the Vietnam War at Riverside Church in New York. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1967-06-12 | Loving v. Virginia unanimously strikes down a state ban on interracial marriage. — [Jim Crow laws](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/jim-crow-laws.md) |
| 1967-07-23 | A police raid on an unlicensed bar on 12th Street sets off five days of unrest in which 43 people die. — [Detroit](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/detroit.md) |
| 1967-10-02 | Thurgood Marshall is sworn in as the first African American justice. — [Supreme Court of the United States](../articles/constitution-and-government/supreme-court-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1967-11 | Announces the Poor People's Campaign. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1968 | A new Civil Rights Act prohibits discrimination in housing. — [Jim Crow laws](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/jim-crow-laws.md) |
| 1968 | About 1,300 Black sanitation workers in AFSCME Local 1733 strike in Memphis for 65 days. — [Labor movement](../articles/economy-and-work/labor-movement.md) |
| 1968 | Avery v. Midland County extends the equal-population rule to elected local governments. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1968 | Dennis Banks, Clyde Bellecourt and George Mitchell found the American Indian Movement in Minneapolis. — [Native Americans](../articles/native-nations/native-americans.md) |
| 1968 | Pigmeat Markham's comic, funk-based "Here Comes the Judge" anticipates later rap boasting. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1968 | The Fair Housing Act follows the Chicago Freedom Movement's campaign against discriminatory housing in northern cities. — [Great Migration](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/great-migration.md) |
| 1968-03 | Oil companies announce the Prudhoe Bay discovery on the North Slope. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1968-04-03 | Gives his last speech at Bishop Charles J. Mason Temple in Memphis while supporting striking sanitation workers. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1968-04-04 | Assassinated on a balcony of the Lorraine Motel in Memphis. — [Martin Luther King Jr.](../articles/people-who-shaped-america/martin-luther-king-jr.md) |
| 1968-04-04 | Martin Luther King Jr. is assassinated in Memphis, Tennessee. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1968-04-11 | Johnson signs the Civil Rights Act of 1968, known as the Fair Housing Act. — [Civil rights movement](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/civil-rights-movement.md) |
| 1968-05-06 | Oklahoma governor Dewey Bartlett launches the OKIE campaign to turn the old insult into a badge of state pride. — [Okies](../articles/society-and-people/okies.md) |
| 1969-02 | NASA reaches agreement with Lick Observatory, leaving only months to prepare the telescope and laser. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1969-07-15 | Ralph Abernathy leads hundreds of Poor People's Campaign demonstrators demanding aid for Americans facing hunger and poverty. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1969-07-16 – 1969-07-24 | **[Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md)** (Event) |
| 1969-07-16 | A Saturn V rocket lifts Apollo 11 off Launch Pad 39A at Cape Kennedy, Florida, at 9:32 a.m. EDT. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1969-07-19 | An engine burn behind the Moon places Columbia and Eagle in lunar orbit. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1969-07-20 | Eagle lands at 4:17 p.m. EDT; Armstrong steps onto the surface at 10:56 p.m. EDT. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1969-07-21 | **[Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md)** (Event) |
| 1969-07-21 | Apollo 11 astronauts set the reflector array on the lunar surface about 20 feet from the lunar module. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1969-07-21 | Armstrong and Aldrin lift off from the Moon, leaving instruments including a laser retroreflector. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1969-07-24 | Columbia splashes down in the Pacific and the crew is recovered by USS Hornet. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1969-08-01 | Lick Observatory's 120-inch telescope detects the first laser returns from the array. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1969-08-03 | A faster-firing laser at Lick records 98 range measurements in one night. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1969-08-20 | McDonald Observatory in Texas makes its first acquisition measurements of the array. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1970 | Congress extends Section 5 preclearance for five years. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1970 | Frank James (Wamsutta), an Aquinnah Wampanoag, speaks on Cole's Hill in Plymouth on Thanksgiving Day, beginning the annual National Day of Mourning. — [Thanksgiving](../articles/culture-and-arts/thanksgiving.md) |
| 1970 | Only about half of Black Americans still live in the South, most of them now in urban areas. — [Great Migration](../articles/gilded-age-and-progressive-era/great-migration.md) |
| 1970 | State park ranger Alexander Weiss finds Chinese poems carved into the detention barracks walls, which were slated for demolition. — [Angel Island Immigration Station](../articles/society-and-people/angel-island-immigration-station.md) |
| 1970 | The Nixon administration repudiates termination and turns federal policy toward tribal self-determination. — [Tribal sovereignty](../articles/native-nations/tribal-sovereignty.md) |
| 1970 | The Soviet Luna 17 mission lands the Lunokhod 1 rover carrying a reflector array. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1970-05 | State park ranger Alexander Weiss finds hundreds of poems in the barracks, then slated for demolition. — [Chinese detention-barracks poetry](../articles/culture-and-arts/chinese-detention-barracks-poetry.md) |
| 1970-06-22 | President Richard Nixon signs Voting Rights Act amendments that lower the voting age to 18 in all elections, while doubting Congress's power to do so by statute. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1970-07-08 | President Richard Nixon asks Congress to repudiate termination and adopt a policy of self-determination. — [Native Americans](../articles/native-nations/native-americans.md) |
| 1970-12-21 | In Oregon v. Mitchell a divided Supreme Court upholds the 18-year-old vote for federal elections but strikes it down for state and local elections. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1971 | After a national tour, the command module Columbia goes to the Smithsonian. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1971 | Apollo 14 and Apollo 15 crews deploy two more U.S. arrays, the second with 300 prisms. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1971 | Gil Scott-Heron records "The Revolution Will Not Be Televised," a spoken-word model for socially conscious rap. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1971 | President Nixon ends foreign central banks' ability to exchange dollars for gold, breaking the dollar's last link to gold. — [Economic history of the United States](../articles/economy-and-work/economic-history-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1971 | Reed v. Reed applies heightened review to a sex-based classification for the first time. — [Equal protection](../articles/rights-and-law/equal-protection.md) |
| 1971-01-21 | Senate Joint Resolution 7, proposing the amendment, is introduced. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1971-03-10 | The Senate passes the resolution unanimously. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1971-03-23 | The House approves it and the amendment is submitted to the states. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1971-07-01 | **[Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md)** (Law) |
| 1971-07-01 | Ratification by three-fourths of the states is completed. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1971-07-05 | GSA Administrator Robert Kunzig certifies the amendment at a White House ceremony attended by Nixon and three 18-year-old witnesses. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1971-12-18 | President Nixon signs the Alaska Native Claims Settlement Act. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1972 | The first Strategic Arms Limitation Talks produce the Anti-Ballistic Missile Treaty and an interim cap on ICBMs, a high point of détente. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1972-09-10 | A memorial boulder and state marker are dedicated at Lattimer; Cesar Chavez speaks. — [Lattimer massacre](../articles/economy-and-work/lattimer-massacre.md) |
| 1973 | A second Soviet reflector reaches the Moon aboard Lunokhod 2. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 1973 | Coleman A. Young is elected Detroit's first African American mayor. — [Detroit](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/detroit.md) |
| 1973 | Mahan v. Howell upholds a state legislative map with a 16.4 percent total deviation, allowing more leeway than for congressional districts. — [Reynolds v. Sims](../articles/democracy-and-elections/reynolds-v-sims.md) |
| 1973-08-11 | **[Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md)** (Tradition) |
| 1973-08-11 | DJ Kool Herc plays the "Back to School Jam" organized by his sister Cindy Campbell at 1520 Sedgwick Avenue in the Bronx. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1974 | Domestic workers are brought under the law. — [Minimum wage](../articles/economy-and-work/minimum-wage.md) |
| 1975 | Congress extends preclearance and updates the formula for the last time, using 1972 election data. — [Shelby County v. Holder](../articles/democracy-and-elections/shelby-county-v-holder.md) |
| 1975 | Congress extends preclearance for seven years and adds protections for language-minority citizens. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1975 | The House abolishes its Committee on Internal Security, HUAC's successor; the Civil Service Commission ends its ban on gay federal employees. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1975 | The Indian Self-Determination and Education Assistance Act lets tribes contract to run programs formerly run by the BIA and the Indian Health Service. — [Native Americans](../articles/native-nations/native-americans.md) |
| 1976 | Columbia goes on permanent display in the new National Air and Space Museum. — [Apollo 11](../articles/science-and-innovation/apollo-11.md) |
| 1976 | Craig v. Boren sets the intermediate-scrutiny standard for sex classifications. — [Equal protection](../articles/rights-and-law/equal-protection.md) |
| 1976 | For the Bicentennial the bell moves from Independence Hall to a glass-and-brick pavilion a block away. — [Liberty Bell](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/liberty-bell.md) |
| 1976 | Mathews v. Eldridge adopts a three-factor balancing test for the process due in civil proceedings. — [Due process](../articles/rights-and-law/due-process.md) |
| 1976 | The Federal Land Policy and Management Act declares that remaining public-domain land will generally stay in federal ownership. — [The West](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/the-west.md) |
| 1976 | The Jann and Yee manuscripts are given to researchers, who use them to recover damaged or missing poems. — [Chinese detention-barracks poetry](../articles/culture-and-arts/chinese-detention-barracks-poetry.md) |
| 1977-06-20 | Oil enters the 800-mile trans-Alaska pipeline at Prudhoe Bay. — [Alaska](../articles/states-and-territories/alaska.md) |
| 1977-09-07 | Jimmy Carter and Omar Torrijos sign the Panama Canal Treaty and the Neutrality Treaty. — [Panama Canal](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/panama-canal.md) |
| 1978 | Amendments to the act strengthen environmental safeguards and coastal-state involvement and reinforce the five-year leasing program. — [Bureau of Ocean Energy Management](../articles/geography-and-environment/bureau-of-ocean-energy-management.md) |
| 1978 | United States v. Wheeler describes tribal powers as inherent; Oliphant v. Suquamish Indian Tribe denies tribes criminal jurisdiction over non-Indians. — [Tribal sovereignty](../articles/native-nations/tribal-sovereignty.md) |
| 1978 | Yellowstone becomes the first U.S. site on the UNESCO World Heritage List. — [Yellowstone National Park](../articles/geography-and-environment/yellowstone-national-park.md) |
| 1978-03-16 | The Senate approves the Neutrality Treaty, 68 to 32. — [Panama Canal](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/panama-canal.md) |
| 1978-04-18 | The Senate approves the Panama Canal Treaty by the same 68-to-32 margin. — [Panama Canal](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/panama-canal.md) |
| 1978-11-07 | Voters approve a constitutional amendment making English and Hawaiian the state's official languages. — [Hawaii](../articles/states-and-territories/hawaii.md) |
| 1979 | Attorneys ask the federal court to reopen the Topeka case to test whether segregation's effects remained (Brown III). — [Brown v. Board of Education](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/brown-v-board-of-education.md) |
| 1979 | In Symm v. United States the Supreme Court summarily affirms a ruling against a Texas registrar's residency questionnaire aimed at college students. — [Twenty-sixth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/twenty-sixth-amendment.md) |
| 1979 | The Fatback Band releases "King Tim III (Personality Jock)"; about two months later the Sugarhill Gang's "Rapper's Delight" becomes the first hip hop song on the Billboard Hot 100; Kurtis Blow signs with Mercury, the first rapper on a major label. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1979 | The Soviet invasion of Afghanistan ends détente and stalls the SALT II treaty. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1979-10-01 | The Panama Canal Zone ceases to exist. — [Panama Canal](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/panama-canal.md) |
| 1980 | Him Mark Lai, Genny Lim and Judy Yung publish Island, with translations of 135 poems. — [Chinese detention-barracks poetry](../articles/culture-and-arts/chinese-detention-barracks-poetry.md) |
| 1980-03 | Year-over-year consumer price inflation peaks at close to 15 percent. — [Economic history of the United States](../articles/economy-and-work/economic-history-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1981 | The Cold Crush Brothers face the Fantastic Five at Harlem World in an early landmark rap battle. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1981 | The Omnibus Budget Reconciliation Act consolidates federal grants as part of the new federalism's push to return programs to the states. — [Federalism](../articles/constitution-and-government/federalism.md) |
| 1981-09-25 | Sandra Day O'Connor is sworn in as the first woman justice. — [Supreme Court of the United States](../articles/constitution-and-government/supreme-court-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1982 | Afrika Bambaataa's "Planet Rock" and Grandmaster Flash and the Furious Five's "The Message" are released. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1982 | Congress renews the special provisions for 25 years and amends Section 2 so plaintiffs need not prove discriminatory purpose, responding to Mobile v. Bolden (1980). — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 1982 | UNESCO inscribes Cahokia Mounds State Historic Site on the World Heritage List. — [Cahokia](../articles/native-nations/cahokia.md) |
| 1982-01-19 | Secretarial Order 3071 establishes the Minerals Management Service, which later combines offshore leasing, safety oversight and royalty collection. — [Bureau of Ocean Energy Management](../articles/geography-and-environment/bureau-of-ocean-energy-management.md) |
| 1982-11 | A severe recession that followed the Federal Reserve's anti-inflation policy ends; the Great Inflation era closes. — [Economic history of the United States](../articles/economy-and-work/economic-history-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1983 | The first Chesapeake Bay Agreement creates the Chesapeake Executive Council. — [Chesapeake Bay](../articles/geography-and-environment/chesapeake-bay.md) |
| 1983 | The restored barracks open to the public as a museum in Angel Island State Park. — [Angel Island Immigration Station](../articles/society-and-people/angel-island-immigration-station.md) |
| 1983 | The restored barracks open to the public as a museum in Angel Island State Park. — [Chinese detention-barracks poetry](../articles/culture-and-arts/chinese-detention-barracks-poetry.md) |
| 1983-04-12 | Harold Washington wins the general election with 51.8 percent, becoming the city's first Black mayor. — [Chicago](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/chicago.md) |
| 1983-11-10 | A federal court in San Francisco vacates Fred Korematsu's conviction, citing government misconduct. — [Japanese American incarceration during World War II](../articles/depression-and-world-war/japanese-american-incarceration.md) |
| 1984 | Former residents of Chenega resettle at Chenega Bay on Evans Island, twenty years after the tsunami. — [1964 Alaska earthquake](../articles/geography-and-environment/1964-alaska-earthquake.md) |
| 1984 | The "Montgomery GI Bill" creates an education program for the all-volunteer force, funded partly by a pay reduction during a recruit's first year. — [Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/servicemens-readjustment-act-of-1944.md) |
| 1984 | Video Music Box becomes the first music-video program devoted to hip hop. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1984-03-22 | Mississippi, which had rejected the amendment in 1920, becomes the last state to ratify it. — [Nineteenth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/nineteenth-amendment.md) |
| 1985 | The Interior Department designates the dam a National Historic Landmark. — [Hoover Dam](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/hoover-dam.md) |
| 1985-05-13 | A bomb dropped on the MOVE house on Osage Avenue kills eleven people and burns more than 60 homes. — [Philadelphia](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/philadelphia.md) |
| 1985-10 | Ronald Reagan and Mikhail Gorbachev hold their first summit, in Geneva. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1986 | Run-DMC's Raising Hell becomes the first hip hop album certified platinum. — [Hip hop](../articles/culture-and-arts/hip-hop.md) |
| 1986 | The Bureau of Engraving and Printing makes the master die from which current dies are produced. — [Great Seal of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/great-seal-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1986 | The last remaining homestead entries are eliminated. — [The West](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/the-west.md) |
| 1986-10-07 | Congress designates the rose as the national floral emblem. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1987 | California v. Cabazon Band of Mission Indians holds that state regulation of on-reservation bingo would infringe tribal government. — [Tribal sovereignty](../articles/native-nations/tribal-sovereignty.md) |
| 1987 | Patricia Nelson Limerick publishes The Legacy of Conquest. — [The West](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/the-west.md) |
| 1987-12-11 | John Philip Sousa's The Stars and Stripes Forever becomes the national march. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1988 | Western Union sells off its telecommunications assets to concentrate on money transfers. — [Electric telegraph](../articles/science-and-innovation/telegraph.md) |
| 1988 | Wildfires burn about 36 percent of the park. — [Yellowstone National Park](../articles/geography-and-environment/yellowstone-national-park.md) |
| 1988 | World War II merchant mariners are finally recognized as veterans, decades after Roosevelt asked Congress to extend them similar benefits. — [Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/servicemens-readjustment-act-of-1944.md) |
| 1988-08-10 | President Ronald Reagan signs the Civil Liberties Act of 1988, with an apology and $20,000 for each surviving incarcerated person. — [Japanese American incarceration during World War II](../articles/depression-and-world-war/japanese-american-incarceration.md) |
| 1988-10-21 | Congress agrees to H. Con. Res. 331, acknowledging the contributions of the Iroquois Confederacy and other Indian nations to the formation of the United States. — [Haudenosaunee](../articles/native-nations/haudenosaunee.md) |
| 1989 | The wampum belts taken in 1890 are returned to the Onondaga Nation. — [Haudenosaunee](../articles/native-nations/haudenosaunee.md) |
| 1989-06-21 | In Texas v. Johnson the Supreme Court holds that burning a flag in political protest is protected expression. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 1989-11 | The Berlin Wall falls, and Communist rule collapses across Eastern Europe. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1990 | Huffman Prairie Flying Field is designated a National Historic Landmark. — [Huffman Prairie Flying Field](../articles/science-and-innovation/huffman-prairie.md) |
| 1991-07-17 | The building, renamed the Brown Building, is designated a National Historic Landmark. — [Triangle Shirtwaist Factory fire](../articles/economy-and-work/triangle-shirtwaist-factory-fire.md) |
| 1991-08 | Hard-line Communists stage a failed coup against Gorbachev. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1991-12-25 | Gorbachev resigns as Soviet president and the Soviet flag is lowered over the Kremlin for the last time. — [Cold War](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/cold-war.md) |
| 1992 | In Department of Commerce v. Montana the Supreme Court upholds the equal-proportions method. — [Census and apportionment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/census-and-apportionment.md) |
| 1992 | New York v. United States begins a line of decisions barring Congress from commandeering state governments. — [Federalism](../articles/constitution-and-government/federalism.md) |
| 1992-05-07 | A pay amendment proposed in 1789 is ratified as the Twenty-seventh Amendment. — [United States Constitution](../articles/constitution-and-government/united-states-constitution.md) |
| 1992-05-07 | The second of the twelve 1789 articles, on congressional pay, is ratified as the Twenty-seventh Amendment. — [Bill of Rights](../articles/rights-and-law/bill-of-rights.md) |
| 1992-10-16 | The Dayton Aviation Heritage Preservation Act makes the field part of a new national historical park. — [Huffman Prairie Flying Field](../articles/science-and-innovation/huffman-prairie.md) |
| 1993 | A major summer flood strikes the upper river basin. — [Mississippi River](../articles/geography-and-environment/mississippi-river.md) |
| 1993-11-23 | Congress apologizes for the U.S. role in the 1893 overthrow (Public Law 103-150). — [Hawaii](../articles/states-and-territories/hawaii.md) |
| 1994-04-04 | Jamestown Rediscovery begins excavating and soon finds that James Fort survives on dry land. — [Jamestown Colony](../articles/colonial-and-revolutionary/jamestown.md) |
| 1994-08-23 | Congress designates December 7 as National Pearl Harbor Remembrance Day. — [Attack on Pearl Harbor](../articles/depression-and-world-war/attack-on-pearl-harbor.md) |
| 1994-10-20 | The Equity in Educational Land-Grant Status Act makes tribal colleges and universities land-grant institutions. — [Morrill Land-Grant College Act](../articles/science-and-innovation/morrill-land-grant-college-act.md) |
| 1995-01-12 | The first of 31 gray wolves captured in Canada are brought into the park, restoring a species killed off by 1926. — [Yellowstone National Park](../articles/geography-and-environment/yellowstone-national-park.md) |
| 1995-04-26 | United States v. Lopez strikes down the Gun-Free School Zones Act of 1990 as beyond the Commerce Clause, 5 to 4. — [Federalism](../articles/constitution-and-government/federalism.md) |
| 1995-07 | The National Security Agency begins releasing the Venona decrypts of wartime Soviet intelligence cables. — [McCarthyism](../articles/cold-war-and-civil-rights/mccarthyism.md) |
| 1996 | Congress passes the Defense of Marriage Act, defining marriage for federal purposes as between a man and a woman and letting states refuse to recognize other states' same-sex marriages. — [Obergefell v. Hodges](../articles/contemporary-america/obergefell-v-hodges.md) |
| 1997 | Mary Moran meets Bendu Jabati of Sierra Leone, linked by a song Turner recorded in the 1930s. — [Gullah Geechee](../articles/society-and-people/gullah-geechee.md) |
| 1997 | The City Council exonerates Catherine O'Leary and her cow of starting the 1871 fire. — [Chicago](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/chicago.md) |
| 1997 | The immigration station is designated a National Historic Landmark. — [Chinese detention-barracks poetry](../articles/culture-and-arts/chinese-detention-barracks-poetry.md) |
| 1997 | The site is designated a National Historic Landmark. — [Angel Island Immigration Station](../articles/society-and-people/angel-island-immigration-station.md) |
| 1997-09-10 | The centennial is marked with new state markers and remarks in the Congressional Record. — [Lattimer massacre](../articles/economy-and-work/lattimer-massacre.md) |
| 1998-04-24 | A tree-ring study in Science shows Jamestown was founded during the driest seven years in 770 years (1606–1612). — [Starving Time of 1609–1610](../articles/colonial-and-revolutionary/starving-time.md) |
| 1998-04-30 | After eight days of debate, the Senate approves the accession of Poland, Hungary and the Czech Republic, 80 to 19. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 1998-07-21 | The National Underground Railroad Network to Freedom Act (Public Law 105-203) creates a National Park Service commemoration program. — [Underground Railroad](../articles/civil-war-and-reconstruction/underground-railroad.md) |
| 1999 | Al Qaeda leader Usama Bin Ladin approves the airliner plot conceived by Khalid Sheikh Mohammed (late 1998 or early 1999). — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 1999-12-31 | The United States transfers the canal to Panama. — [Panama Canal](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/panama-canal.md) |
| 2000 | A Save America's Treasures grant funds restoration of the station, including the poems. — [Chinese detention-barracks poetry](../articles/culture-and-arts/chinese-detention-barracks-poetry.md) |
| 2000 | About 1.9 percent of employed Americans work in agriculture. — [Economic history of the United States](../articles/economy-and-work/economic-history-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 2000 | Rice v. Cayetano holds that the amendment protects all persons and that ancestry can serve as a proxy for race. — [Fifteenth Amendment](../articles/democracy-and-elections/fifteenth-amendment.md) |
| 2001-01 | The Hart-Rudman Commission proposes a National Homeland Security Agency to consolidate nearly two dozen agencies. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2001-07 | By early July all 19 hijackers, who had begun entering the country in January 2000, are inside the United States. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2001-09-11 | **[September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md)** (Event) |
| 2001-09-11 | Hijacked airliners strike the World Trade Center's North Tower (8:46 a.m.) and South Tower (9:03 a.m.) and the Pentagon (9:37 a.m.); United 93 crashes in Pennsylvania at 10:03 a.m. after a passenger and crew revolt. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2001-09-12 | NATO invokes Article 5 for the first time, the day after the September 11 attacks on the United States. — [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/nato.md) |
| 2001-09-18 | Congress's Authorization for Use of Military Force against those responsible for the September 11 attacks, and those who harbored them, is approved. — [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/war-in-afghanistan.md) |
| 2001-09-18 | President George W. Bush signs the Authorization for Use of Military Force against those responsible. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2001-10 | Bush signs the USA PATRIOT Act, expanding surveillance and security powers. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2001-10-07 – 2021-08-30 | **[War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/war-in-afghanistan.md)** (Conflict) |
| 2001-10-07 | U.S. air strikes against Taliban and al Qaeda targets in Afghanistan begin Operation Enduring Freedom. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2001-10-07 | U.S. air strikes begin against Taliban military installations and al Qaeda training camps. — [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/war-in-afghanistan.md) |
| 2001-10-08 | Executive Order 13228 creates the White House Office of Homeland Security and the Homeland Security Council. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2001-10-11 | Senator Joseph Lieberman introduces S. 1534, an early bill for a homeland security department. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2001-12-07 | Hamid Karzai's southern opposition forces and U.S. Marines take Kandahar after the Taliban abandon Kabul and the north. — [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/war-in-afghanistan.md) |
| 2002-05-30 | The recovery effort at Ground Zero formally ends with the removal of the Last Column. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2002-06-06 | President George W. Bush, reversing his earlier position, proposes a Cabinet-level department in an address to the nation. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2002-07-26 | The House passes H.R. 5005, 295–132. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2002-11-13 | Congress reenacts the national-motto statute without changing its wording. — [E pluribus unum](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/e-pluribus-unum.md) |
| 2002-11-19 | The Senate passes the amended bill, 90–9; the House agrees to it on November 22. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2002-11-25 | Bush signs the Homeland Security Act of 2002 (Public Law 107-296). — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2003 | The U.S. Senate marks the centennial of Handy's Tutwiler encounter by declaring 2003 the Year of the Blues. — [Blues](../articles/culture-and-arts/blues.md) |
| 2003 | The bell moves to a new building at Sixth and Market Streets, beside the site where Washington held enslaved people in the 1790s. — [Liberty Bell](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/liberty-bell.md) |
| 2003-01-24 | **[Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md)** (Institution) |
| 2003-01-24 | The act takes effect and the department comes into existence. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2003-03-01 | FEMA and 21 other organizations formally join the department. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2003-03-01 | The Department of Homeland Security opens, combining parts or all of 22 federal agencies. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2003-08-11 | NATO assumes command of the UN-mandated International Security Assistance Force (ISAF). — [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/war-in-afghanistan.md) |
| 2003-11-18 | Massachusetts's Supreme Judicial Court rules 4–3 in Goodridge v. Department of Public Health that the state's ban on same-sex marriage is unconstitutional. — [Obergefell v. Hodges](../articles/contemporary-america/obergefell-v-hodges.md) |
| 2004 | The bipartisan 9/11 Commission issues its final report and closes on August 21, 2004. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2004-02-14 | The Mississippi African-American Monument is dedicated in Vicksburg National Military Park. — [Siege of Vicksburg](../articles/civil-war-and-reconstruction/siege-of-vicksburg.md) |
| 2004-05-17 | Goodridge takes effect and Massachusetts becomes the first state where same-sex couples can marry. — [Obergefell v. Hodges](../articles/contemporary-america/obergefell-v-hodges.md) |
| 2004-12-08 | Congress designates the oak as the national tree. — [National symbols of the United States](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/national-symbols-of-the-united-states.md) |
| 2005 | Secretary Michael Chertoff's Second Stage Review leads to a major internal reorganization. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2005 | The Energy Policy Act extends offshore authority to renewable energy such as wind, wave and ocean-current power. — [Bureau of Ocean Energy Management](../articles/geography-and-environment/bureau-of-ocean-energy-management.md) |
| 2005-07 | The APOLLO project at Apache Point Observatory, New Mexico, begins ranging with millimetre-level precision. — [Lunar Laser Ranging Experiment](../articles/science-and-innovation/lunar-laser-ranging-experiment.md) |
| 2005-08-31 | After levee breaches during Hurricane Katrina, at least 80 percent of the city lies under floodwater. — [New Orleans](../articles/regions-cities-and-landmarks/new-orleans.md) |
| 2005-10-26 | NIST issues its final report on the collapse of the Twin Towers. — [September 11 attacks](../articles/contemporary-america/september-11-attacks.md) |
| 2006 | Congress establishes the Captain John Smith Chesapeake National Historic Trail. — [Chesapeake Bay](../articles/geography-and-environment/chesapeake-bay.md) |
| 2006 | Congress renews preclearance for 25 years, by votes of 390 to 33 in the House and 98 to 0 in the Senate, without changing the formula. — [Shelby County v. Holder](../articles/democracy-and-elections/shelby-county-v-holder.md) |
| 2006 | Congress renews the special provisions for 25 more years in the Fannie Lou Hamer, Rosa Parks, Coretta Scott King, Cesar E. Chavez, Barbara Jordan, William Velazquez and Dr. Hector Garcia reauthorization act. — [Voting Rights Act of 1965](../articles/democracy-and-elections/voting-rights-act-of-1965.md) |
| 2006 | The housing boom peaks and residential construction begins to fall; home prices peak early the next year. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2006-10-04 | Bush signs the Post-Katrina Emergency Management Reform Act, reorganizing FEMA after Hurricane Katrina. — [Department of Homeland Security](../articles/contemporary-america/department-of-homeland-security.md) |
| 2006-10-12 | The National Heritage Areas Act of 2006 designates the Gullah Geechee Cultural Heritage Corridor and its federal commission. — [Gullah Geechee](../articles/society-and-people/gullah-geechee.md) |
| 2007-08 | Strains appear in the market for asset-backed commercial paper as mortgage losses mount. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2007-09-03 | Panama breaks ground on its own Third Set of Locks Project, reusing the Atlantic-side excavations. — [Third Locks Project](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/third-locks-project.md) |
| 2007-12 – 2009-06 | **[Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md)** (Event) |
| 2007-12 | The recession begins, according to the National Bureau of Economic Research. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-03-24 | The Federal Reserve Bank of New York announces financing for JPMorgan Chase's acquisition of Bear Stearns. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-09-07 | The Federal Housing Finance Agency places Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac in government conservatorship. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-09-15 | Lehman Brothers files for bankruptcy. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-09-16 | The New York Fed agrees to lend up to $85 billion to the insurer AIG. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-09-29 | The House rejects the first version of the bank rescue bill, 205–228. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-10-03 | President George W. Bush signs the Emergency Economic Stabilization Act, creating the Troubled Asset Relief Program. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2008-10-14 | Congress lets veterans and service members out of uniform give the military salute during the anthem. — [The Star-Spangled Banner](../articles/symbols-and-national-identity/star-spangled-banner.md) |
| 2008-12 | The Federal Reserve cuts its federal funds rate target to a range of 0 to 0.25 percent. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2009 | In Northwest Austin the Court lets a Texas utility district seek bailout and avoids the constitutional question while voicing serious doubts about the Act. — [Shelby County v. Holder](../articles/democracy-and-elections/shelby-county-v-holder.md) |
| 2009-02-17 | President Barack Obama signs the American Recovery and Reinvestment Act. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2009-05-07 | Regulators publish the results of the first bank stress tests. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2009-06 | The recession ends, after 18 months. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2009-07-24 | The federal minimum rises to $7.25 an hour, its rate since. — [Minimum wage](../articles/economy-and-work/minimum-wage.md) |
| 2009-08 | The Osage Nation buys Sugarloaf Mound in St. Louis, the city's last surviving mound. — [Cahokia](../articles/native-nations/cahokia.md) |
| 2009-09 | Pauketat's article "America's First Pastime" argues that chunkey helped organize Cahokian social and political life. — [Chunkey](../articles/native-nations/chunkey.md) |
| 2009-09-09 | President Obama addresses a joint session of Congress to press for health care reform. — [Affordable Care Act](../articles/contemporary-america/affordable-care-act.md) |
| 2009-10 | Unemployment peaks at 10.0 percent. — [Great Recession](../articles/contemporary-america/great-recession.md) |
| 2009-12-01 | In a speech at West Point, President Obama approves a surge of 33,000 more U.S. troops, with withdrawals to begin in July 2011. — [War in Afghanistan (2001–2021)](../articles/foreign-relations-and-military/war-in-afghanistan.md) |
| 2009-12-24 | The Senate passes H.R. 3590, as amended, by 60 to 39. — [Affordable Care Act](../articles/contemporary-america/affordable-care-act.md) |
| 2010 | McDonald v. City of Chicago applies the Second Amendment to the states. — [Bill of Rights](../articles/rights-and-law/bill-of-rights.md) |
| 2010 | Shelby County, Alabama, sues the Attorney General in federal court in Washington, D.C., to have Sections 4(b) and 5 declared unconstitutional. — [Shelby County v. Holder](../articles/democracy-and-elections/shelby-county-v-holder.md) |
| 2010 | University of Maryland archaeologists and local volunteers survey the massacre site with metal detectors. — [Lattimer massacre](../articles/economy-and-work/lattimer-massacre.md) |
| 2010-03-21 | The House agrees to the Senate amendments, 219 to 212, with no Republican votes in favor. — [Affordable Care Act](../articles/contemporary-america/affordable-care-act.md) |
| 2010-03-23 | **[Affordable Care Act](../articles/contemporary-america/affordable-care-act.md)** (Law) |
| 2010-03-23 | Obama signs the Patient Protection and Affordable Care Act. — [Affordable Care Act](../articles/contemporary-america/affordable-care-act.md) |
